# Amharic review priorities

Generated by `python3 scripts/review_priorities.py am --write`; do not edit by hand. Built from the Amharic renderings in `content/` (30636 records), for the native reviewers who start with [the Amharic review guide](../amharic-review-guide.md). Every record is `ai_draft`: ticking a box here changes nothing. A reviewer approves or flags a sentence in the site admin's Review page, and a choice settled differently is changed in its terms doc and in every passage drafted under it.

Links go to the verse on the site. Verse numbers are those of the Hebrew and Greek, as in `content/`; where English Bibles number a verse differently, the English number follows in brackets. Levels are L (Literal), B (Balanced), R (Readable).

1. [Uncertain decisions](#1-uncertain-decisions)
2. [Passages to read first](#2-passages-to-read-first)
3. [New name forms to confirm](#3-new-name-forms-to-confirm)
4. [Possible closeness to older Amharic Bibles](#4-possible-closeness-to-older-amharic-bibles)

## 1. Uncertain decisions

392 Amharic rendering decisions are marked uncertain: 340 in the Old Testament and 52 in the New, in 41 books. Each gives the verse, the levels, the category, the choice, the alternatives the drafter weighed and a question to answer. Answer it in the Review page's note (approve if the drafted wording stands; flag it with the wording you want if not).

### Genesis (ዘፍጥረት) (15)

- [ ] [Genesis 9:6](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/9/#v6) L/B · word_choice · "መልክ" for the image of God
  - Alternatives: "አምሳል"
  - Why: "መልክ" (form, likeness) is the plain modern word for an image that resembles its original; "አምሳል" is the older church word. Readable says what it means, that people are made to be like God.
  - **Question:** Is «መልክ» right here, or is «አምሳል» better?
- [ ] [Genesis 21:33](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/21/#v33) L/B/R · word_choice · "የአጣጥ ዛፍ" for the tamarisk
  - Alternatives: a loanword such as "ታማሪስክ"; "ዛፍ" (a tree) only
  - Why: The Hebrew names a particular hardy desert tree (meaning decision m1). "የአጣጥ ዛፍ" is the name Amharic readers know for it from Bible reading; a reviewer should confirm that it is still the current name of the tree.
  - **Question:** Which Amharic word, if any, do readers know for this, and is the drafted one clear?
- [ ] [Genesis 22:22](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/22/#v22) L/B/R · word_choice · "ኬሴድ፣ ሐዞ፣ ፊልዳሥ፣ ይድላፍ" (and in 22:21, 24 "ዖፅ፣ ቡዝ፣ ቀሙኤል፣ ሬማ፣ ጥባሕ፣ ገሐም፣ ተሐሽ፣ ማዕካ") written from the Hebrew sounds
  - Alternatives: the forms of a particular Amharic Bible
  - Why: These names appear only here; they are spelled from the Hebrew, and a reviewer should bring them into line with the forms Amharic readers know where those differ.
  - **Question:** Are these the spellings Amharic readers know, or the right forms for new names?
- [ ] [Genesis 24:62](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/24/#v62) L/B/R · word_choice · "ብኤርላሃይሮኢ" for Beer-lahai-roi
  - Alternatives: a translated name such as "የሚያየኝ ሕያው ጉድጓድ"
  - Why: The well was named in 16:14; the name is written as a name, in the same form wherever it appears in Genesis. A reviewer should check it against the form used in Genesis 16.
  - **Question:** Is «ብኤርላሃይሮኢ» the spelling Amharic readers know, or the right form for a new name?
- [ ] [Genesis 30:14](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/30/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "ማንድራጎራ" for the mandrakes; Readable adds "የሚባል ተክል" (a plant called)
  - Alternatives: "እንኮይ"; "የፍቅር ፍሬ"
  - Why: The plant does not grow in Ethiopia and has no settled Amharic name. "እንኮይ" names a different local fruit and would mislead; "የፍቅር ፍሬ" (love fruit) explains rather than names. The plant's international name is used, with the meaning decision (m1) saying what …
  - **Question:** Which Amharic word, if any, do readers know for this, and is the drafted one clear?
- [ ] [Genesis 30:32](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/30/#v32) L/B/R · word_choice · "ዝንጉርጉር" (speckled), "ባለ ነጠብጣብ" (spotted), "ጥቁር" (dark), and in verse 35 "ባለ ሸንተረር" (striped), through chapters 30 and 31
  - Alternatives: "ዥጉርጉር" for speckled
  - Why: Each Hebrew colour word keeps one Amharic word through the story, so the reader can follow which animals were Jacob's (31:8-12).
  - **Question:** Which Amharic word, if any, do readers know for this, and is the drafted one clear?
- [ ] [Genesis 30:37](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/30/#v37) L/B/R · word_choice · "ልብን" (poplar), "ለውዝ" (almond), "አርሞን" (plane tree)
  - Alternatives: "ዋርካ" for the plane tree
  - Why: "ለውዝ" is the Amharic almond. Poplar and plane tree have no common Amharic names; "ልብን" and "አርሞን" follow the Hebrew names, and "ዋርካ" would name a fig tree, a different tree.
  - **Question:** Which Amharic word, if any, do readers know for this, and is the drafted one clear?
- [ ] [Genesis 31:10](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/31/#v10) L/B · word_choice · "ባለ ጠቃጠቆ" (blotched) for mottled
  - Alternatives: "ባለ ነጠብጣብ"
  - Why: A word apart from "ባለ ነጠብጣብ" (spotted, 30:32), since the Hebrew word is different (m1); Readable joins it to "ነጠብጣብ", as the English Readable says "spots".
  - **Question:** Is «ባለ ጠቃጠቆ» right here, or is «ባለ ነጠብጣብ» better?
- [ ] [Genesis 31:47](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/31/#v47) L/B/R · word_choice · "ይጋር ሠሀዱታ" and "ገልዒድ" for the two names
  - Alternatives: "ገለዓድ" for Galeed
  - Why: The names follow the sounds of the Aramaic and the Hebrew. "ገልዒድ" is kept apart from "ገለዓድ" (Gilead, verse 21) so the reader can tell the heap from the region, while the likeness the meaning decision notes still shows.
  - **Question:** Is «ይጋር ሠሀዱታ» the spelling Amharic readers know, or the right form for a new name?
- [ ] [Genesis 32:31 (English 32:30)](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/32/#v31) L/B/R · word_choice · "ጵኒኤል" here and "ፋኑኤል" for Penuel in verse 32 (L, B); Readable keeps "ጵኒኤል"; Readable explains the name as «የእግዚአብሔር ፊት»
  - Alternatives: none given
  - Why: The two Hebrew spellings are kept apart at Literal and Balanced, in their usual Ethiopian forms (m2 of verse 32); Readable keeps one, as the English does. The explanation uses "ፊት" (face), the word of Jacob's saying.
  - **Question:** Are these the spellings Amharic readers know, or the right forms for new names?
- [ ] [Genesis 34:12](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/34/#v12) L/B/R · word_choice · "ጥሎሽ" for the bride-price (mohar); "ስጦታ" for the gift (mattan)
  - Alternatives: "የሙሽራ ዋጋ"
  - Why: "ጥሎሽ" is the Amharic word for the payment a marriage brings, the nearest current word for the mohar paid by the groom (m1); a reviewer should check that readers will not take it as the bride's dowry.
  - **Question:** Is «ጥሎሽ» right here, or is «የሙሽራ ዋጋ» better?
- [ ] [Genesis 34:13](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/34/#v13) L/B/R · word_choice · "ተንኮል" (deceit) for mirmah at every level
  - Alternatives: "ማታለል"
  - Why: One word for the trick, so that it can match the word used where Isaac says Jacob came "with deceit" (27:35); the lead should check that chapter 27 has "በተንኮል" too.
  - **Question:** Is «ተንኮል» right here, or is «ማታለል» better?
- [ ] [Genesis 36:24](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/36/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "ፍል ውኃ" (hot springs)
  - Alternatives: "ውኃ" (water) alone; a transliteration of the Hebrew word
  - Why: The Hebrew word occurs only here and is uncertain (see the meaning decision); "hot springs" is the likely sense, and "ፍል ውኃ" is the everyday Amharic word for a hot spring.
  - **Question:** Is «ፍል ውኃ» right here, or is «ውኃ» better?
- [ ] [Genesis 41:45](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/41/#v45) L/B/R · word_choice · names: "ጻፍናት ፐዕናህ", "አስናት", "ጶጢፋራ" (Potiphera), "ኦን"
  - Alternatives: "ጲጥፋራ" for Potiphera
  - Why: Potiphera is kept apart from Potiphar ("ጲጥፋራ", the terms list), as the Hebrew keeps the two names apart; the Egyptian name is written as it sounds. A reviewer may prefer other established forms.
  - **Question:** Are these the spellings Amharic readers know, or the right forms for new names?
- [ ] [Genesis 43:11](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/43/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "በለሳን" (balm), "ሙጫ" (gum), "ከርቤ" (myrrh), "ፒስታቺዮ" (pistachio nuts), "ለውዝ" (almonds)
  - Alternatives: "ኦቾሎኒ" (peanuts); "ዕጣን" (frankincense)
  - Why: The exact plants are uncertain; these are the nearest Amharic names, and "ፒስታቺዮ" is borrowed because Amharic has no word of its own. A reviewer may know better names.
  - **Question:** Which Amharic word, if any, do readers know for this, and is the drafted one clear?

### Exodus (ዘጸአት) (20)

- [ ] [Exodus 3:14](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/3/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "እኔ ያለሁ ነኝ" for "I AM WHO I AM"; "አለሁ" (I AM) used as a name: "አለሁ ወደ እናንተ ልኮኛል" (L, B), "አለሁ የሚባለው እርሱ" (the one called I AM, R)
  - Alternatives: "እኔ የሆንኩት ነኝ" (I am what I am); "የምሆነውን እሆናለሁ" (I will be what I will be); "ያለና የሚኖር እኔ ነኝ" (the 1962 Bible's wording)
  - Why: As the English (r-en-1) and the terms list ask for this verse, the phrase is built from the verb "to be" and kept in the present, with "I will be" given in the footnote (m1). Amharic has two verbs of being, "አለ" (to be, to exist, to be present) and the copula …
  - **Question:** Are these the spellings Amharic readers know, or the right forms for new names?
- [ ] [Exodus 4:18](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/4/#v18) L/B/R · register · Moses speaks to his father-in-law with "አንተ" ("እባክህ")
  - Alternatives: "እርስዎ" ("እባክዎ")
  - Why: Between kin in the Pentateuch the familiar form is used; a reviewer may prefer the respectful form for a son-in-law to his wife's father, which many Amharic speakers would use.
  - **Question:** Is the register right for each level (close at Literal, fit for public reading at Balanced, plain at Readable), and can it be read aloud in church?
- [ ] [Exodus 6:3](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/6/#v3) L/B/R · setting_alternative · El Shaddai as "ሁሉን የሚችል አምላክ" (God Almighty); the name as "{{divine_name:እግዚአብሔር|ያህዌ}} በሚለው ስሜ" (by my name, the LORD / Yahweh)
  - Alternatives: {{divine_name:በእግዚአብሔር|በያህዌ}} ስሜ
  - Why: The Pentateuch terms list sets "ሁሉን የሚችል አምላክ" for El Shaddai, the title of the promises (m2). The verse follows the English and its divine_name span. With the default, an Amharic reader meets "እግዚአብሔር", the word Amharic also uses for God in general (1:1), …
  - **Question:** Do both options of the setting read naturally here, and is the default the right one?
- [ ] [Exodus 6:14](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/6/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · "የአባቶቻቸው ቤቶች" (their fathers' houses, L), "ቤተሰቦች" (families, B, R); "ጎሳ" for the clan; the names in their established Amharic forms (ሐኖክ, ፈሉ, ኤስሮን, ከርሚ and through the list)
  - Alternatives: none given
  - Why: As the Pentateuch terms list sets for the units of a tribe (m1). The names of the list follow the forms Amharic readers know from Genesis 46 and the Ethiopian Bible tradition; Korah's son Assir is "አሲር", kept apart from Asher ("አሴር").
  - **Question:** Are these the spellings Amharic readers know, or the right forms for new names?
- [ ] [Exodus 6:15](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/6/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · Simeon's son Shaul is "ሳኡል"
  - Alternatives: "ሳኦል"
  - Why: The New Testament books keep "ሳኦል" for the king and "ሳውል" for Paul; Simeon's son is given a third form so that he is not read as either.
  - **Question:** Is «ሳኡል» right here, or is «ሳኦል» better?
- [ ] [Exodus 23:15](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/23/#v15) R · supplied_words · በአቢብ ወር፣ በመጋቢትና በሚያዝያ አካባቢ (R)
  - Alternatives: በፀደይ መጀመሪያ (in early spring), as the English Readable says
  - Why: Ethiopian seasons do not fall as the English "spring" does, so Readable names the Ethiopian months in which Abib falls, March to April, to place the feast for an Amharic reader.
  - **Question:** Are the supplied words needed, and are they natural Amharic?
- [ ] [Exodus 25:5](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/25/#v5) L/B/R · word_choice · "የተለፋ ጥሩ ቆዳ" (fine tanned leather) for the tachash skins, following the English "fine leather"
  - Alternatives: "የአቆስጣ ቆዳ"; "የባሕር እንስሳ ቆዳ"
  - Why: The animal is not known (see the meaning decision); plain words for good leather avoid naming one, as in Exodus 35-40.
  - **Question:** Which Amharic word, if any, do readers know for this, and is the drafted one clear?
- [ ] [Exodus 25:7](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/25/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "ኦኒክስ" for the shoham stone
  - Alternatives: "መረግድ"; "ሰርዶንክስ"
  - Why: The stone is not certainly known; "መረግድ" is already the emerald of Exodus 28:18 and Revelation 21:19, so the common loanword is used.
  - **Question:** Which Amharic word, if any, do readers know for this, and is the drafted one clear?
- [ ] [Exodus 26:15](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/26/#v15) L/B/R · word_choice · "ሳንቃዎች" (boards, panels) for the qerashim, standing upright
  - Alternatives: "ቋሚ ክፈፎች" (upright frames); "ተራዳዎች" (upright posts)
  - Why: The word used in the carrying out (Exodus 36-40), so the two halves read alike. The meaning decision prefers open frameworks, which "ሳንቃ" (a broad board) does not show; the boards of the bronze altar (27:8), a different Hebrew word, are "ጣውላዎች".
  - **Question:** Is «ሳንቃዎች» right here, or is «ቋሚ ክፈፎች» better?
- [ ] [Exodus 26:17](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/26/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "ምላሶች" (tongues) for the tenons, the Hebrew "hands", at every level
  - Alternatives: "እጆች" at Literal; "ችንካሮች" (pegs) at Readable
  - Why: "ምላስ" is the carpenter's word for a tongue that fits a slot; "እጆች" would read as arms of the frame.
  - **Question:** Is «ምላሶች» right here, or is «እጆች» better?
- [ ] [Exodus 27:10](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/27/#v10) L/B/R · word_choice · "ማሰሪያዎች" for the bands (chashuqim) of the pillars
  - Alternatives: "ዘንጎች" (rods); "ቀለበቶች"
  - Why: The meaning decision leaves open whether they ran around or between the pillars; "ማሰሪያ" (a band that binds) fits both, and "ቀለበቶች" is kept for the rings.
  - **Question:** Is «ማሰሪያዎች» right here, or is «ዘንጎች» better?
- [ ] [Exodus 28:4](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/28/#v4) L/B · word_choice · "በዳማ ሽመና" (in a checkered weave) for the tunic's pattern
  - Alternatives: "ዥንጉርጉር"
  - Why: "ዳማ" is a pattern of squares; "ዥንጉርጉር" means spotted.
  - **Question:** Is «በዳማ ሽመና» right here, or is «ዥንጉርጉር» better?
- [ ] [Exodus 28:17](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/28/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · The twelve stones take the Amharic names of Revelation 21:19-20 where the English names agree (ሰርድዮን, ቶጳዝዮን, ቢረሌ, ሰንፔር, መረግድ, ያክንት, አሜቴስጢኖስ, ክርስቲሎቤ, ኢያስጲድ); loanwords for the rest (ቱርኩዋዝ, አጌት, ኦኒክስ); …
  - Alternatives: modern gem names throughout

  - Why: A general word that does not decide between the walls, the woodwork or the whole building, all of which have been proposed.

- [ ] [Ezra 6:2](https://openwordbible.org/am/ezr/6/#v2) L/B/R · word_choice · "አሕምታ" for Ecbatana, the city's name as the Aramaic gives it; "ሜዶን" for Media
  - Alternatives: "ኤክባታና" (the Greek form)
  - Why: The English uses the Greek form its readers know; the Amharic Bible tradition knows the city by its Aramaic name, and the Greek form is no more familiar to Amharic readers. Media is "ሜዶን", as the Medes are "ሜዶናውያን" in Acts 2:9.
  - **Question:** Is «አሕምታ» the spelling Amharic readers know, or the right form for a new name?
- [ ] [Ezra 7:24](https://openwordbible.org/am/ezr/7/#v24) L/B/R · word_choice · "ግብር፣ ቀረጥና የመንገድ ቀረጥ" (tribute, tax and toll; R "ምንም ዓይነት ግብር")
  - Alternatives: none given
  - Why: Three ordinary Amharic words for a land tribute, a levy and a road toll, as in 4:13, 20.
  - **Question:** Is «ግብር፣ ቀረጥና የመንገድ ቀረጥ» accurate and natural here, or is there a better Amharic word?
- [ ] [Ezra 8:17](https://openwordbible.org/am/ezr/8/#v17) L/B/R · word_choice · "ኢዶ" for this Iddo, kept apart from "አዶ", the prophet Zechariah's grandfather (5:1; 6:14)
  - Alternatives: "አዶ" for both
  - Why: The Hebrew spells the two names differently.
  - **Question:** Is «ኢዶ» the spelling Amharic readers know, or the right form for a new name?
- [ ] [Ezra 9:6](https://openwordbible.org/am/ezr/9/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "በደል" (L), "ኃጢአት" (B, R) for iniquity (ʿawon); "ጥፋት" (guilt) for the Hebrew ʾashmah at every level, here and through chapters 9-10
  - Alternatives: "በደል" for both
  - Why: Keeping two words apart lets the prayer's pairing of sin and guilt stay visible; "ጥፋት" here is culpability, as in "ጥፋተኛ".
  - **Question:** Is «በደል» right here, or is «በደል» better?
- [ ] [Ezra 10:3](https://openwordbible.org/am/ezr/10/#v3) L/B/R · word_choice · "ጌታ" (the Lord) for the word the vowels read as the divine title
  - Alternatives: "ጌታዬ" (my lord, Ezra)
  - Why: As the English; the consonants could also be read "my lord", meaning Ezra, which the meaning decision records.
  - **Question:** Is «ጌታ» right here, or is «ጌታዬ» better?
- [ ] [Ezra 10:4](https://openwordbible.org/am/ezr/10/#v4) L/B/R · register · Shecaniah and the assembly (verse 12) speak to Ezra with the respectful "እርስዎ" and the polite imperative ("ይነሡ", "ይበርቱ")
  - Alternatives: "አንተ" ("ተነሥ", "በርታ")
  - Why: Ezra is the priest and the king's envoy; a layman addressing him in public would use the respectful forms, as subjects do to a ruler. Ezra speaks to the people with "እናንተ".
  - **Question:** Is the register right for each level (close at Literal, fit for public reading at Balanced, plain at Readable), and can it be read aloud in church?

  - Alternatives: "የሰሊሆም ኩሬ" (the pool of Siloam, John 9:7); "የሼላሕ ኩሬ"

  - Alternatives: "የምፍቃድ በር", keeping the Hebrew name

  - Alternatives: "እያንዳንዱ መሣሪያው ውኃው" word for word

  - Alternatives: "እንደምትለው"

  - Alternatives: none given

  - **Question:** Which Amharic word, if any, do readers know for this, and is the drafted one clear?

  - Alternatives: "ሞኝነት" for ʾivvelet
  - Why: "ስንፍና" is the noun of "ሰነፍ", the terms list's word for the fool ʾewil, so the fool and his folly share a root as in the Hebrew; "ሞኝነት" is kept for kesilut, the folly of the kesil ("ሞኝ"). "ተግሣጽ" is the terms list's word for musar, correction that trains.

  - Alternatives: "ዋኔ"
  - Why: The second bird is a swift or swallow, a fast, darting flier; the Amharic bird name is a proposal for a reviewer to check.

  - Alternatives: "ነፍሱን ይሻሉ"
  - Why: In Amharic "ነፍሱን ይሻሉ" (they seek his life) can only mean they want to kill him; the meaning decision reads that the upright care for the blameless one's life, so Literal uses the Hebrew verb's other sense, to ask after.
  - **Question:** Is «ስለ ነፍሱ ይጠይቃሉ» right here, or is «ነፍሱን ይሻሉ» better?

  - Alternatives: "ኢትኤል", "ዑካል"
  - Why: Agur son of Jakeh as the terms list. Ithiel and Ucal are read as two names, as the meaning decision has it, and transcribed from the Hebrew; they occur nowhere else in the Amharic so far (Ithiel of Nehemiah 11:7 is not yet drafted and should match).

  - Why: The meaning decision says probably a gecko; the general word is used, since the Hebrew word differs from the one Leviticus 11:30 renders የቤት እንሽላሊት.

  - Why: እንዝርት is the spindle of Ethiopian hand-spinning; Amharic has no common single word for the distaff, so መፍተያ (the spinning tool) is used.
  - **Question:** Is «መፍተያ» right here, or is «ፈትል መጠቅለያ» better?

  - Alternatives: "መንፈስ" (the breath of life)
  - Why: The meaning decision hears both wind and the breath of life here; Amharic follows the English "wind", the picture the line draws, with "ነፋስ" as in "ነፋስን መከተል".
  - **Question:** Is «ነፋስ» right here, or is «መንፈስ» better?

  - Alternatives: "ፍላጎት" at every level
  - Why: ʾaviyyonah is the caper-berry, eaten to stir appetite (the meaning decision: appetite and desire fade). The plant has no common Amharic name; Literal names it, Balanced says what it does, Readable gives the sense, as the English Readable.

- [ ] [Song of Songs 1:14](https://openwordbible.org/am/sng/1/#v14) L/B/R · word_choice · henna "እንሶስላ"; En-gedi "ዓይንጋዲ" (as 1 Samuel 24)
  - Alternatives: "ሂና"

  - Alternatives: "ጽጌረዳ" (rose)
  - Why: The flower is a small wild flower of the meadows (meaning decision), not a garden rose; Amharic has no common name for the crocus, so it is called what it is. The familiar "rose of Sharon" is not followed.

  - Alternatives: "ዕርድ" (turmeric) for saffron

  - **Question:** Is this wording accurate and natural Amharic, or should it change?
- [ ] [John 8:28](https://openwordbible.org/am/jhn/8/#v28) L/B/R · word_choice · እኔ እንደ ሆንኩ (that I am), as in 8:24
  - Alternatives: እኔ እርሱ እንደ ሆንኩ (that I am he)
  - Why: The Greek has 'I am' with no predicate. Amharic እኔ እንደ ሆንኩ ('that I am') can stand without one, as the Greek does, so the reader meets the bare words and can hear the echo of God's self-naming the meaning decision describes; supplying እርሱ ('he') would settle …
  - **Question:** Is this wording accurate and natural Amharic, or should it change?
- [ ] [John 8:58](https://openwordbible.org/am/jhn/8/#v58) L/B/R · word_choice · እኔ አለሁ (I am, I exist) at every level, set against ከመገኘቱ (came to be, L), ከመኖሩ (was, B), ከመወለዱ (was born, R)
  - Alternatives: እኔ ነበርኩ (I was); እኔ ነኝ (it is I)
  - Why: The present verb of being after a past 'before' is as surprising in Amharic as in Greek, and keeps the contrast between Abraham's beginning and Jesus's being; እኔ ነበርኩ would make it a claim of age only, and እኔ ነኝ needs a predicate. Whether Amharic readers will …
  - **Question:** Is this wording accurate and natural Amharic, or should it change?

### Acts (የሐዋርያት ሥራ) (4)

- [ ] [Acts 8:30](https://openwordbible.org/am/act/8/#v30) L/B/R · register · እርስዎ forms from Philip to the official (ያስተውሉታልን, ያውቃሉን, ይረዱታል); አንተ forms from the official to Philip (እለምንሃለሁ, እባክህ)
  - Alternatives: አንተ forms both ways, as the Greek singular; እርስዎ forms both ways
  - Why: Philip, a stranger on foot, speaks to a high official of a royal court, and in Amharic he would use the respectful እርስዎ, as subjects do to rulers elsewhere; the official answers with the plain አንተ he would use to a younger man. The narrative keeps the plain …
  - **Question:** Is the register right for each level (close at Literal, fit for public reading at Balanced, plain at Readable), and can it be read aloud in church?
- [ ] [Acts 16:6](https://openwordbible.org/am/act/16/#v6) L/B/R · word_choice · "ቢቲኒያ" for Bithynia
  - Alternatives: "ቢታንያ", the spelling some Amharic Bibles use
  - Why: "ቢታንያ" is the established spelling of Bethany near Jerusalem in the Gospels, so using it for this Roman province in Asia Minor would make two different places look the same. A reviewer should confirm the form.
  - **Question:** Is «ቢቲኒያ» the spelling Amharic readers know, or the right form for a new name?
- [ ] [Acts 16:11](https://openwordbible.org/am/act/16/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "ኔአጶሊስ" for Neapolis
  - Alternatives: "አዲስ ከተማ" (new city), translating the name; "ናጱሊ"
  - Why: The name is spelled out from the Greek, which writes it as two words, "New City"; it is the port of Philippi, and translating it would hide that it is a name. A reviewer should confirm the form readers know.
  - **Question:** Is «ኔአጶሊስ» the spelling Amharic readers know, or the right form for a new name?
- [ ] [Acts 20:16](https://openwordbible.org/am/act/20/#v16) L/B/R · word_choice · በዓለ ሃምሳ (the Feast of Fifty) for Pentecost
  - Alternatives: ጰንጠቆስጤ (the Greek name spelled out)
  - Why: Pentecost was the Festival of Weeks, fifty days after Passover (m1); በዓለ ሃምሳ is the name the Ethiopian church calendar gives it and says what the Greek name means, while ጰንጠቆስጤ is now heard mostly as the name of a church movement.
  - **Question:** Is this wording accurate and natural Amharic, or should it change?

### Romans (ሮሜ) (16)

- [ ] [Romans 15:18](https://openwordbible.org/am/rom/15/#v18) L/B/R · word_choice · "እልዋሪቆን" for Illyricum
  - Alternatives: "ኢሊሪኮም", closer to the Latin name
  - Why: This follows the form of the name in the Ethiopian Bible tradition as best recalled here; the spelling should be checked by an Amharic reviewer against the established form.
  - **Question:** Is «እልዋሪቆን» the spelling Amharic readers know, or the right form for a new name?
- [ ] [Romans 15:23](https://openwordbible.org/am/rom/15/#v23) L/B/R · word_choice · "እስጳንያ" for Spain
  - Alternatives: "ስፔን", the modern name of the country
  - Why: The Bible tradition's form of the Roman province's name is used, so Paul's world is not made modern; a reviewer may prefer the modern "ስፔን" at Readable.
  - **Question:** Is «እስጳንያ» the spelling Amharic readers know, or the right form for a new name?
- [ ] [Romans 16:7](https://openwordbible.org/am/rom/16/#v7) L/B/R · word_choice · "አንድሮኒቆስ" (Andronicus), "ዩልያን" (Junia)
  - Alternatives: a spelling closer to the Greek or Latin form
  - Why: These are the forms of the Amharic Bible tradition. The meaning decision reads Junia as a woman; "ዩልያን" does not show gender in itself, and it is close to "ዩልያ" (Julia, verse 15), whose object form is also "ዩልያን". A reviewer may prefer "ዩንያ", nearer the Greek.
  - **Question:** Is «አንድሮኒቆስ» accurate and natural here, or is there a better Amharic word?
- [ ] [Romans 16:8](https://openwordbible.org/am/rom/16/#v8) L/B/R · word_choice · "አምጵልያጦስ" (Ampliatus)
  - Alternatives: a spelling closer to the Greek or Latin form
  - Why: The name is given in the form it is thought to have in the Ethiopian Bible tradition, or, where no form is known, spelled from the Greek in the way that tradition spells such names. The spelling is not certain and should be checked by an Amharic reviewer.
  - **Question:** Is «አምጵልያጦስ» the spelling Amharic readers know, or the right form for a new name?

  - Alternatives: a spelling closer to the Greek or Latin form

  - Alternatives: a spelling closer to the Greek or Latin form

  - **Question:** Is «ኤርስጦስ» the spelling Amharic readers know, or the right form for a new name?

### 1 Corinthians (1 ቆሮንቶስ) (1)

- [ ] [1 Corinthians 1:8](https://openwordbible.org/am/1co/1/#v8) R · supplied_words · እግዚአብሔር (God) named as the subject at Readable
  - Alternatives: እርሱ (he), as Literal and Balanced
  - Why: Amharic Readable needs a plain subject for a new short sentence. The meaning decision reads "who" as most likely God (verses 4 and 9), so Readable names him; Literal and Balanced keep the open "እርሱ".
  - **Question:** Are the supplied words needed, and are they natural Amharic?

### 2 Thessalonians (2 ተሰሎንቄ) (1)

- [ ] [2 Thessalonians 2:3](https://openwordbible.org/am/2th/2/#v3) L/B · word_choice · ክህደቱ / ታላቁ ክህደት (the rebellion, the great falling away, L, B); ብዙዎች በእግዚአብሔር ላይ ካላመፁ (unless many rebel against God, R)
  - Alternatives: ዓመፁ (the revolt)
  - Why: The meaning decision reads the word as a known, final revolt against God; "ክህደት" is turning against the God one owed loyalty, and keeps it apart from "ዓመፃ" (unrighteousness, verses 10 and 12). Readable says plainly what happens, as the English Readable does.
  - **Question:** Is this wording accurate and natural Amharic, or should it change?

### Revelation (የዮሐንስ ራእይ) (3)

- [ ] [Revelation 14:8](https://openwordbible.org/am/rev/14/#v8) L/B · word_choice · "ምኞት" (passion, L) and "የሚያሳብድ" (maddening, B) for Babylon's wine; "ቁጣ" (fury) for God's wine in 14:10
  - Alternatives: "ቁጣ" in both places
  - Why: The Greek uses one word for the heat of Babylon's immorality and for God's fury that answers it. Amharic has no word that carries both, so the sense is given in each place; the reader loses the wordplay but not the meaning.
  - **Question:** Is «ምኞት» right here, or is «ቁጣ» better?
- [ ] [Revelation 18:11](https://openwordbible.org/am/rev/18/#v11) L/B/R · word_choice · "ሽታ ያለው እንጨት" (scented wood) for citron wood
  - Alternatives: a transliterated tree name
  - Why: The Greek names the North African citron (thyine) tree, unknown in Ethiopia; what mattered was its costly fragrance and grain, so Amharic describes it, as several translations do.
  - **Question:** Which Amharic word, if any, do readers know for this, and is the drafted one clear?
- [ ] [Revelation 21:19](https://openwordbible.org/am/rev/21/#v19) L/B/R · word_choice · the stones by their traditional Amharic names: ኢያስጲድ, ሰንፔር, ኬልቄዶን, መረግድ, ሰርዶንክስ, ሰርድዮን, ክርስቲሎቤ, ቢረሌ, ቶጳዝዮን, ክርስጵራስስ, ያክንት, አሜቴስጢኖስ
  - Alternatives: modern loanwords from English (ጃስፐር, ኤመራልድ, ...); descriptions by colour
  - Why: As the English keeps the traditional names, the Amharic uses the names long current in Ethiopian church use (from the Greek through Ge'ez). Several of the ancient stones cannot be identified with certainty, and the Amharic spellings of the rarer ones need a …
  - **Question:** Which Amharic word, if any, do readers know for this, and is the drafted one clear?

## 2. Passages to read first

43 passages, in the order to read them: first the passages readers know best, where the wording matters most and where memory of the older Bibles pulls hardest; then the passages the drafting found hardest. Read each at all three levels, with the English and the meaning decisions beside it (the verse inspector, or the reader's Study view). The counts are the passage's records, its Amharic rendering decisions and the uncertain ones among them.

## 3. New name forms to confirm

Names the drafters had no earlier book to copy from, so they made a new Amharic form, mostly from the Hebrew. Confirm the spelling, or give the form Amharic readers know. Counts are the verses whose text uses the form as a word (with or without a case prefix or suffix); "also written" lists spellings that differ only in letters that sound alike (ሀ ሐ ኀ ሃ, ሰ ሠ, አ ዐ ኣ ዓ, ጸ ፀ), which may be the same name spelled two ways, or a different name or an ordinary word.

## 4. Possible closeness to older Amharic Bibles

113 decisions mention an older Amharic Bible (the 1954 E.C. / 1962 Bible and its revisions), familiar wording, or wording changed to avoid it. Open the passage beside the older Bible: the drafts must be their own wording, with nothing longer than a short fixed phrase in common, and a form the drafters kept because readers know it (a name, a term) should be one that is free to use. Lines from the terms notes come first.

### From the terms notes

- docs/amharic-style.md:22: - **Never reproduce the wording of a copyrighted Amharic Bible**: the 1954
- docs/amharic-style.md:23: E.C. (1962) Bible and its 1980 revision (Bible Society of Ethiopia), the
- docs/amharic-style.md:25: the Easy-to-Read Version and others. The 1962 Bible shapes how most readers
- docs/amharic-style.md:28: sentence that matches a well-known version for more than a short common
- docs/amharic-style.md:253: - **Behold** (*idou*) is እነሆ at Literal, left out or reworded at Balanced
- docs/amharic-style.md:278: anything longer that matches the 1962 Bible is reworded.
- docs/pentateuch-amharic-terms.md:15: Bible, and never reproduce the wording of an Amharic Bible (1962, 1980,
- docs/pentateuch-amharic-terms.md:17: Exodus 20, Deuteronomy 6) pull hardest: draft from the gloss and reword any
- docs/pentateuch-amharic-terms.md:18: sentence that matches a well-known version beyond a short fixed phrase.
- docs/pentateuch-amharic-terms.md:166: - **"Be fruitful and multiply"**: ተዋለዱ፣ ብዙ (ይዋለዱ፣ ይብዙ), not the 1962 Bible's

### Genesis (ዘፍጥረት) (5)

- [ ] [Genesis 1:1](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/1/#v1) L/B/R · "ሰማያት" (heavens, plural) at Literal; "ሰማይ" (heaven, sky) at Balanced and Readable, through the book — Balanced reads as the older Amharic versions do here because this short sentence has only one natural Amharic wording.
- [ ] [Genesis 1:22](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/1/#v22) L/B/R · "ተዋለዱ፣ ብዙ" (breed and multiply) at Literal and Balanced, here and in verse 28 (and 9:1, 9:7); "ብዙ ተዋለዱ" (breed much) at Readable — The familiar "ብዙ ተባዙ" of older versions is not used.
- [ ] [Genesis 2:13](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/2/#v13) L/B/R · "ኩሽ" (Cush) at every level — Older Amharic Bibles say Ethiopia here, following the Greek translation.
- [ ] [Genesis 8:17](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/8/#v17) L/B · "ይዋለዱ፣ ይብዙ" for "be fruitful and multiply" — Amharic says each one plainly ("ተዋለዱ", "ብዙ"), and the same pair is used for the people in 9:1 and 9:7, rather than the familiar doubled wording of older Amharic Bibles.
- [ ] [Genesis 9:1](https://openwordbible.org/am/gen/9/#v1) L/B · "ተዋለዱ፣ ብዙ" for "be fruitful and multiply" — As in 8:17: the Hebrew's two verbs, to bear children and to become many, are said plainly, here and in 9:7, rather than in the familiar doubled wording of older Amharic Bibles.

### Exodus (ዘጸአት) (4)

- [ ] [Exodus 3:14](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/3/#v14) L/B/R · "እኔ ያለሁ ነኝ" for "I AM WHO I AM"; "አለሁ" (I AM) used as a name: "አለሁ ወደ እናንተ ልኮኛል" (L, B), "አለሁ የሚባለው እርሱ" (the one called I AM, R) — The wording leans toward "I am the one who is" more than the Hebrew does, and the 1962 Bible's familiar form was not followed; Alternative: "ያለና የሚኖር እኔ ነኝ" (the 1962 Bible's wording)
- [ ] [Exodus 7:16](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/7/#v16) L/B/R · the demand, the same at each occurrence: "ሕዝቤን ልቀቅ፤ ያገልግሉኝ" (Literal), "እንዲያገለግሉኝ ሕዝቤን ልቀቅ" (Balanced), "እንዲያመልኩኝ ሕዝቤን ልቀቅ" (Readable) — "ያገለግሉኝ ዘንድ" is the wording of the older Amharic Bible.
- [ ] [Exodus 20:1](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/20/#v1) L/B/R · The Ten Words drafted from the Hebrew, in wording of their own — Alternative: The familiar wording of the 1962 Amharic Bible
- [ ] [Exodus 39:10](https://openwordbible.org/am/exo/39/#v10) L/B/R · The stones take the names Revelation 21:19-20 gives the same stones in this translation ("ሰርድዮን" carnelian, "ቶጳዝዮን" topaz, "ቢረሌ" beryl, "ሰንፔር" sapphire, "መረግድ" … — Alternative: new names drawn from the 1962 Bible

- [ ] [Numbers 6:24](https://openwordbible.org/am/num/6/#v24) L/B/R · Drafted from the Hebrew, three lines each naming the LORD (m3): "ይባርክህ ... ይጠብቅህም" (L), "ይንከባከብህም" (B, watch over you), "መልካም ያድርግልህ ... ከክፉም ሁሉ ይጠብቅህ" (R, do … — Balanced and Readable follow the English levels' sense (m2: guard, watch over, protect) in their own words, and the lines of 6:25-26 are worded independently of the familiar 1962 version.
- [ ] [Numbers 24:17](https://openwordbible.org/am/num/24/#v17) L/B/R · a star will march out: "ኮከብ ተራምዶ ይወጣል" (L), "ኮከብ ገሥግሦ ይወጣል" (B), "ኮከብ ይወጣል" (R); scepter "ንጉሣዊ በትር" — The verse is drafted from the Hebrew and reworded away from the familiar Amharic wording.

- [ ] [Deuteronomy 31:10](https://openwordbible.org/am/deu/31/#v10) L/B/R · "የመልቀቂያው ዓመት" (L, B) for the year of release; "ዕዳ በሚሰረዝበት ዓመት" (R) — "የምሕረት ዓመት" is familiar from older Amharic Bibles but now suggests a year of mercy in general.
